HDI concerns easing
New capacity and price hikes boosting earnings visibility for 2027-28
We are increasingly confident in Gold Circuit’s transition towards higher-layer-
count HDI boards, supported by meaningful investments in technology, talent, and
new capacity. The planned capacity ramp aligns well with the next-generation

ASIC schedules of two major CSP customers, while potential layer-count increase
from 26 layers for Trainium3 to 34 layers for Trainium4 should drive meaningful
content growth. Meanwhile, PCB price hikes and improving cost pass-through
should support a sequential GM recovery from 3Q26. We raise our 2027/28 EPS
forecasts by 1%/9% and lift our TP from NT$1,955 to NT$2,000 while reiterating
our High Conviction Outperform rating.
Less concerned about the HDI transition

While the market remains concerned about Gold Circuit’s HDI capabilities, we
believe the company has recruited experienced talent and invested meaningfully in
HDI technology to support future ASIC demand. The second Thailand plant and
new capacity at the first Suzhou plant are scheduled to contribute from 2H27,
followed by the second Suzhou plant in 1H28, with all three expansions primarily
dedicated to HDI production. Importantly, the capacity ramp should align well with

the next-generation ASIC schedules of two major CSP customers. We expect Gold
Circuit to maintain its leading position in AWS Trainium4 and benefit from
meaningful content growth, driven by an increase in PCB layer count from 26 layers
for Trainium3 to 34 layers for Trainium4.
Price hikes underway
For AI accelerator PCBs, we believe Gold Circuit should gradually pass rising CCL
costs through to customers. Our checks suggest that non-AI PCB products are

being quoted at 3-4x prior price levels, with lead-times extending to at least four
months at leading PCB vendors. This indicates a tightening industry supply-demand
environment and suggests that the GM weakness seen in 2Q26 should not persist
in the coming quarters. We forecast GM to improve sequentially to 36.7%/36.8%
in 3Q26/4Q26.
Earnings revisions and valuation
We keep 2026SP EPS largely unchanged but raise our 2027/28 forecasts by 1%/9%
to reflect higher production value contributed by the new plants and increasing HDI

content. Accordingly, we raise our TP from NT$1,955 to NT$2,000, still based on
30x 2H27-1H28SP PE, and reiterate our High Conviction Outperform rating.

### Page 5

HDI concerns easing Gold Circuit - HC O-PF
14 September 2026 5
Investment thesis
We expect Gold Circuit to benefit from the strong CCL/PCB material upgrade trend,
rising shipments of AI servers and continued spec upgrades in CPU servers, which
require higher PCB layer counts. The upgrade in networking towards 800G should
also lead to improving margins for the company.
Catalysts
1) Accelerating server shipments driven by US CSPs and enterprise customers; 2)

server content value growth; 3) a stronger-than-expected networking demand
recovery; 4) a notebook demand recovery.
Valuation details
We value all of our coverage in the PCB/CCL space on PE as it better reflects
margins and earnings outlooks, which we believe the market will mainly focus on.
We use a target PE multiple of 30x, in line with the downstream server sector
average after the recent rerating driven by AI. Given GCE's higher-than-peer
revenue exposure to cloud servers and switches, its leading market position, and

our expectation of robust AI/ASIC server shipments with computing server demand
to gradually recover, we think 30x PE is justified as server/PC ODM suppliers have
all rerated to the 15-30x range on the back of the booming AI demand. And as GCE
also generates a similar number of sales from server and AI, it will likely enjoy strong
growth (better content growth from server and switch) and better returns (less
competition) than assemblers in the mid-to-long term.

Investment risks
Downside risks: (1) order adjustment as a result of chip shortages or supply chain
disruption; (2) weaker-than-expected cloud/datacentre Capex demand; (3) slower
adoption or delayed launch of Intel’s Eagle Stream and AMD’s Genoa platforms; (4)
further raw material price hikes, and (5) more severe competition across the
industry.

上銀(2049) 
買進, TP 420 (潛在報酬 29.83%)  
買進 
Price(09/14)：323.5 
Target Price：420

上銀 26Q2 受惠於產品漲價效益顯現及 UTR 提升，單季毛利率攀
升至 34.17%。單季合併營收達 75.08 億元，QoQ 17.70% 、YoY 
26.71%。營業利益與稅後淨利分別大幅成長至 10.34 億元與 8.48
億元，帶動單季 EPS 攀升至 2.40 元。 
展望後市， 研究部對上銀營收表現維持正向看待 。 目前終端需求持
續暢旺，產品交期維持高位，線性滑軌能見度約 4 至 5 個月，滾
珠螺桿最長可達 6 個月 。 儘管地緣政治因素推升鋼材 、 包材與潤滑
油等原物料價格 ， 但公司具備彈性定價能力且維持多元採購策略，
漲價效益足以抵銷成本上揚。伴隨26Q3 完整反映漲價產品銷售，
預估單季營收達 80.92 億元，QoQ 7.77%，YoY 34.97%，EPS 上
看 2.81 元。放眼 2026 至 2027 年，亞洲半導體需求穩固、歐洲軍
工與航太產業接棒成為新動能 ， 整體訂單需求無虞 。 預估上銀2026
年與 2027 年營收將分別達 296.90 億元與 329.36 億元 ， 維持雙位
數年增幅，YoY 分別為 22.37%與 10.93%。在營運規模提升帶動
下，預估 2026 年、2027 年 EPS 將分別達 9.28 元與 11.87 元。 
評價方面，上銀過去五年 PER 主要集中於 20 倍至 40 倍區間。考
量公司營收與獲利將呈現雙位數成長上升趨勢，產業結構持續上
行，研究部以2027 年預估 EPS 11.87 元作為評價基礎，並給予歷
史區間中緣偏上之 35x 本益比，目標價為 420 元，給予「買進」
之投資建議。  
⚫   ⚫ 公司基本資料 
上銀科技(2049)成立於 1989 年，主要從事線性滑軌(GW)、滾
螺桿(BS)、工業機器人、機器手臂、迴轉工作台、減速機、精
密軸承及醫療設備之研究開發、設計、製造、銷售、維修及售
前售後服務，同時為全球第三大滾珠螺桿廠和線性滑軌廠，僅
次於日本 THK、NSK。公司以自有品牌 「HIWIN」 行銷全球，
海外子公司橫跨德國、美國、日本、瑞士、捷克、法國，

漲價效益逐漸顯現，加上亞洲市場需求延續帶動交期延長；其
中 ， 機器人產品受惠於半導體需求強勁 ， 出貨量年增達 7 成。
第二季合併營收達 75.08 億元 ，QoQ 17.70%，YoY 26.71%。

⚫ 預估營收獲利季增，帶動 26Q3 單季 EPS 達 2.81 元 
上銀 8 月營收 27.08 億元 ，MoM 0.98%，YoY 42.30%；1-8
月累計營收 192.77 億元，YoY 23.03% 。研究部預期，9 月
營收有望延續雙位數年增態勢，預估  26Q3 營收將達 80.92 
億元，QoQ 7.77%，YoY 34.97%。 
獲利表現方面，考量第三季將完整反映產品漲價效益，且營收
規 模 擴 張 帶 動  UTR 提 升 ， 預 估  26Q3 毛 利 率 將 達 
35.43%，季增 1.27 個百分點，年增 6.83 個百分點。預估
單季營業利益達 12.00 億元 ，QoQ 16.11%，YoY 192.40%；
稅後淨利 9.94 億元，QoQ 17.25% ，YoY 122.90% ，單季 
EPS 預估為 2.81 元。

⚫ 全球需求延續，獲利有望持續成長，估 2026 年 EPS 9.28 元 
研究部對上銀後市營收維持正向看待，係因以下：(1) 終端需
求持續暢旺，產品交期維持高位； (2) 公司具備彈性定價能
力，漲價效益足以覆蓋成本上揚；(3) 半導體產業需求穩固，
持續帶動滾珠螺桿與機器人等相關產品出貨。 
訂單能見度方面，目前整體產品交期維持高檔，線性滑軌約  
4-5 個月 ， 滾珠螺桿最長可達 6 個月 。 觀察各區域下游需求：
亞洲市場維持暢旺，其中中國受惠於電動車、電子與半導體產
業持續擴張，帶動製造設備與傳動元件需求；東南亞受惠全球
供應鏈重組，除傳統傳動元件外， 亦間接推升機器人產品需
求；台灣則持續受惠半導體與科技業資本支出，帶動螺桿與機
器人穩健成長。歐洲市場方面，儘管德國汽車工業仍顯疲態，
但軍工與航太產業已成為新成長動能，加上捷克、波蘭等中歐
製造核心需求保持穩定，有效支撐歐洲營運。

公司研究報告

2383台光電(Elite Material Co.)

報 告 日 期 ：2026/09/21 研 究 員： 方姿婷  (Lesley  Fang ) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要：  
⚫ 2Q26 營收 符 合 預 期，毛 利 率 受1H26 漲 價 提 升，預 期 3Q26 毛利
將反應 26 年 6 月 價 格 再 次 調 漲 續 增。 
⚫ AI 伺 服 器 帶 動 高 階 銅 箔 基 板需 求 強 勁，將2026 年 月 產 能 從615
萬 張 上 修 至2027 年底達 930 萬 張 、2028 年 底 月 產 能 達 到1,110
萬張。 2027 年， NVIDIA  Rubin 平 台 將 帶 動CCL M8+ 、M9 材料
出貨攀升， AI ASIC CCL 拉 貨 動 能 呈 現50~60% 高 度 成 長 。 
⚫ 2H26 低軌 S 客戶 V3 版本 新型 火 箭 搭 載 低 軌 衛 星 ， 所 採 用 之 銅
箔 基 板 升 級 至M8 等 級 推 升 材 料 成 本 與PCB 產 值 。

一. 2Q26 營收與財報 符合市場預期， 3Q26 展望正向  
2Q26 台 光 電 營 收472.75 億 元 ，+42.97%QoQ 、+110.03%YoY ， 毛 利
率為 33.85% ， 稅 後 淨 利98.73 億 元 ， 稅 後EPS+27.55 元。 2Q26 稼
動 率 維 持 滿 載 ， 受 惠AI 伺 服 器 需 求 帶 動 ，CCL M7 以 上 產 品 高 階
品 項 營 收 比 重 提 升 至60-65% ， 預 計 2H26 CCL 漲 價 持 續 帶 動 營 運
獲利增長。 3Q26 與 2H26 展望，台光電 7 月 營 收(192.06 億元 )、8
月營收 (201.45 億元 )，3Q26 累 計 達 成 率 約69%，符 合 市 場 預 期。由
於 2026 年 6 月 發 布 之 漲 價 效 應 可 反 應 在3Q26 毛 利 率 ， 預 估 台 光
電 3Q26 營收為 573.32 億 元，+21.27%QoQ ，+127.99%YoY ，毛 利 率
35.08% ， 稅 後 淨 利132.02 億 元 ， 單 季 稅 後EPS+36.85 元。

二. AI 伺服器帶動 CCL/PCB 產值提升，產能擴充 60% 
宏遠投顧觀察 ，AI 伺 服 器 與 先 進 封 裝 等 需 求 日 增，高 階CCL 市場
需求將在 2024 年~2026 年 CAGR 達 26%， 而 高 階CCL 供 給 端 CAGR
則是 7% ， 高 階 市 場 需 求 殷 切 。 高 階 銅 箔 基 板(包含 AI 伺服器和高
階 網 路 交 換 器 ， 自2024 年始採用 M8 CCL 材料； 2026 年 M8 材料
佔總體 CCL 市場 40% ，M8 材 料 佔 比 顯 著 高 於2022 年、 2023 年的
22% 和 26% 。 台 系 銅 箔 基 板 供 應 鏈 當 中 ，M6~M8 等 高 階 材 料 的 供
應廠商為 (2383) 台 光 電。回顧 近年來 台 光 電 產 能 爬 坡 速 度，2025 年
月產能 400~550 萬張、 2026 年 月 產 能615 萬 張 、 預 計2027 年 月 產
能從 700~800 萬張至 4Q27 增至 930 萬 張 、 預 計2028 年 底 月 產 能
達到 1,110 萬 張 ， 連 續4 年 年 產 能 擴 充 近60%。2027 年隨著 NVIDIA  
Rubin 平 台 將 帶 動CCL M8+ 、M9 材 料 出 貨 攀 升 ，AI ASIC CCL 拉
貨動能 呈現 50~60% 高 度 成 長，台 光 電 高 階CCL 新 產 能 釋 出，產 品
組合持續優化 。2027 年 起 ， 除 一 般 型 伺 服 器Intel Diamond Rapids

三. 台光電低軌道衛星板材， 2027 年隨發射進程量增  
台光電於 低 軌 道 衛 星 延 伸 的 商 機，回顧 2025 年 低 軌 衛 星 發 射 量 增
加至 3~3.5 萬顆 (Space X 與 Amazon LEO 發 射 量 ， 年 增 率 達
+45%YoY) ，台 光 電、華 通 等 廠 商 投 入 資 源 開 發 低 軌 道 衛 星PCB 板
材，可 望 受 惠 低 軌 衛 星 鋪 設 建 置 量 增。宏 遠 投 顧 觀 察，低 軌 衛 星 量
體 近年來 成 長 快 速 ，2027 年以前， S 客 戶 進 入 火 箭V2mini 轉換至
V3 版 本 切 換 期(4Q26 開 始 重 啟 新 版 本)，Amazon LEO 發 射 時 程預
計 2027 年 以 前 發 射 量1,500~1,600 顆、 2029 年 發 射 量 達3,200 顆，
長 期 低 軌 衛 星 仍 呈 現 成 長 。天上衛星 PCB 板 技 術 層 次 高 台 光 電 、
華通的市佔率高 )， 天 上 衛 星 PCB 板與相對應之地面接收站量增
長，帶動 2025 ~2027 年 低 軌 衛 星 產 能 足 以 支 應增加 +40~ 50%YoY 拉
貨動能 。低 軌 大 型S 客戶 V3 版 本 新 型 火 箭 搭 載 低 軌 衛 星，所 採 用
之 銅 箔 基 板 升 級 至M8 等 級，材 料 損 耗 要 求 降 低，訊 號 傳 出 完 整 性
標 準 提 高 ， 對 於CCL 、PCB(HDI) 、 玻 纖 與 銅 箔 等 規 格 預 期 同 步 升
級 ， 推 升 材 料 成 本 與PCB 產值。

四.預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 120.77/204.85 元 
2026 年受惠 AI 伺 服 器及 ASIC 需 求 持 續 強 勁 的 帶 動 下 ， 台 光 電 為
銅 箔 基 板 領 導 廠 商 ， 將 受 惠AI Server 對 於 高 階 銅 箔 基 板強 勁 的 需
求 ， 因 而，預估 2026 年 台 光 電 營 收 為1,999.92 億 元 ，+112.17%YoY ，
毛利率 34.21% ，稅 後 淨 利 432.76 億 元，稅 後 EPS+120.77 元。展 望
2027 年，在 AI 伺服器 需 求 持 續 強 勁 的 帶 動 下 ， 帶 動 產 能 持 續 增
長， 預估 2027 年 台 光 電 營 收 為3,530.14 億 元，+76.57%YoY ，毛 利
率為 35.49% ， 稅 後 淨 利734.03 億 元 ， 稅 後EPS+204.85 元。

四.投資建議維持 買進，目標價 6,145 元 
宏 遠 投 顧 認 為，台 光 電營 運 重 點 在 於，營 運 動 能 於2026 年 起 加 速
啟 動，一 般 型 伺 服 器CPU 廠商 Intel 、AMD 均 有 新 品 帶 動 新 平 台 升
級 與 相 關 供 應 的 零 組 件 商 機，以 及 攻 入AI 伺 服 器、高 階 網 路 交 換
器的 CCL 、 低 軌 衛 星CCL 規 格 轉 換 成 長 可 期。 基 於 上 述 ， 給 予台
光電 投 資 建 議 為 買 進 ， 目 標 價6,145 元(30.00x 20 27 EPS) 。

一. 公司簡介  
台光電 (2383) 為 銅 箔 基 板 廠 商，主 要 產 製雙 面 印 刷 電 路 板 與 多 層 印
刷 電 路 板 之 銅 箔 基 板、內 層 銅 箔 基 板、黏 合 片，產 品 應 用 別當 中，
基礎建設佔 75~80%(AI 伺 服 器 佔55~60%、 一 般 型 伺 服 器 佔15~20%、
Switch 佔 15~20% 、LEO 佔 5~10%) 。 行 動 裝 置 佔10~15% 、 車 用 工
業 與 其 他 合 計5~10% 。

程上 HDI 經 過 多 次 壓 合 後，PP 在 熱 壓 多 次後，每 一 層 熱 膨 脹 係 數
不同，導致通孔 Crack 或翹曲，製作時容易產生爆板，因此對於
PCB 板廠製作良率會是一大技術難關，而金像電、健鼎等廠商於
高階 HDI 相 對 具 備 生 產 優 勢 與 競 爭 力 ， 相 對 應 台 光 電 、 台 燿 等 高
階 CCL 供 貨 與 升 級 也 成 為 製 程 一 大 瓶 頸 ；2025 年月產能 400~550
萬張、 2026 年 月 產 能615 萬張、預計 2027 年 月 產 能 從700~800 萬
張至 4Q27 增至 930 萬 張 、 預 計2028 年 底 月 產 能 達 到1,110 萬 張 ，

動 CCL M8+、M9 材 料 出 貨 攀 升 ，AI ASIC CCL 拉 貨 動 能呈現 50~60%
高 度 成 長 ， 台 光 電 高 階CCL 新 產 能 釋 出 ， 產 品 組 合 持 續 優 化。

萬顆上修至 2027 年(V8 增量 )增至 800 萬 顆，倍 數 增 長。AWS ASIC 
預計 2027 年 出 貨 量 達330~350 萬 顆，增 長+21%YoY 。NVIDIA 體系
GPU ，Rubin 系 列 層 數 提 升 至24~26L ， 材 料 端 從M8/M8+ 往 更 高 規
格 升 級 ， 更 高 層 數 與 多 層 式 架 構 ， 促 使PCB 板 訊 號 走 線 、 供 電 完
整性、高速訊號之阻抗匹配控制、訊號傳輸完整性，皆帶動 PCB
與上游材料呈現升級態勢 。 除一般型伺服器 Intel Diamond Rapids

吳昆潮 C00269@ibfs.com.tw 
2481.TT 強茂 
 AI 不泡沫 功率就有夢

[調升評價]   
   公司簡介 
強茂為台灣功率半導體與分離式元件製造大廠，公司採垂直整合佈局，擁有 3 座前段
晶圓製造廠，及 5 座海內外後段封裝測試據點。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
買進 208 
【 前 次 投 資 建 議 】區 間 操 作，150 
【大盤指數/股價】45849 / 168.5

➢ 2Q26 獲利符合預期 
強茂 2Q26 營收 39.56 億元，QoQ+15.62%，YoY+16.34%，毛利率
33.22%， 稅後淨利4.88 億元 ，QoQ+69.58%，YoY+93.85%，EPS 1.28
元，獲利大致符合預期。毛利率維持季增趨勢，但營業費用比預期高，
主因是客戶拉貨強勁，備貨及物流相關費用多及提列獎金所致。 
➢ 下半年營收、獲利逐季向上 
AI 伺服器、車用及小訊號產品訂單能見度佳，強茂 B/B ratio 值維持在
1.3~1.5 高檔位置，3Q26 新增部份產能，加上產品調漲，預期營收及毛
利率持續向上；4Q26 有機會切入另一家 CSP 供應鏈，年底增加封測產
能(小訊號為主)、越南 TOREX 預計在 1Q27 交割，整體而言營運動能無
虞。預估強茂 3Q26 營收 42.51 億元，QoQ+7.47%，YoY+30.02%，
毛利率 34.44%，稅後淨利 5.58 億元，QoQ+14.39%，YoY+84.08%，
EPS 1.46 元。 
➢ 歐美重心轉移、非紅供應鏈，台 IDM 廠有訂價權 
在 AI 需求強勁推動下，單機櫃功耗持續上升並導入高壓直流電架構，帶
動功率元件量價同步提升，應用於伺服器功率元件年複合成長率遠高於
產業平均，在產能吃緊情況下，歐美 IDM 廠將重心轉向利潤較佳的高
壓、SiC 以及低壓大電流的 DrMOS 核心市場，排擠中低壓功率元件產
能。同時，安世、揚傑等中資背景企業面臨去中化，伺服器、車用工控
等領域加速將訂單轉向台廠。 
➢ 預估強茂 2027 年 EPS 6.95 元，建議買進，目標價 208 元 
投研部預估強茂 2026 年營收 159.39 億元，YoY+21.73%，稅後淨利
19.21 億元，YoY+61.22%，EPS 5.03 元；預估 2027 年營收 183.38 億
元，YoY+15.05%，稅後淨利 26.57 億元，YoY+38.29%，EPS 6.95 元。
目前強茂 PE 24X ，2026 年最高本益比已超越 2017~2018 年、
2020~2022 年功率元件景氣循環 20X~30X 區間，AI 伺服器的應用有
助拉升功率元件產業及公司評價，給予強茂買進建議，目標價 208 元
(2027F PE 30X)。

2481 強茂 加權指數

個股報告

公司簡介 
強茂股份有限公司成立於 1986 年，為台灣功率半導體與分離式元件製造大廠，公司
採垂直整合(IDM)佈局，擁有 3 座前段晶圓製造廠(4 吋、6 吋、8 吋各一座)，及 5 座
海內外後段封裝測試據點。 
1H26 產品營收比重 ： 金氧半場效電晶體(MOSFET)佔 32%， 蕭特基二極體(Schottky)
佔 23%，整流器(Rectifier)佔 12%，突波抑制器(TVS)佔 9%，靜電防護二極體(ESD 
Diode)佔 7%，齊納二極體(Zener)佔 6%，開關二極體(Switching)佔 6%，雙極性接
面電晶體(BJT)佔 4%，碳化矽(SiC)佔 1%。若以市場應用來看，車用電子佔營收比重
35%為最大，消費型電子佔 20%，電腦運算佔 16%，工控&綠能佔 13%，電源供應
用佔 12%，通訊佔 4%，其中 AI 人工智能應用佔 11%。 
圖 1：強茂產品組合

資料來源：強茂，國票投顧 
2Q26 獲利符合預期 
強茂 2Q26 營收 39.56 億元，QoQ+15.62%，YoY+16.34%，毛利率 33.22%，稅後
淨利 4.88 億元，QoQ+69.58%，YoY+93.85%，EPS 1.28 元，獲利大致符合預期。
毛利率維持季增趨勢，但營業費用比預期高，主因是客戶拉貨強勁，備貨及物流相關
費用多及提列將金所致。 
資料來源：CMoney，國票投顧

### Page 3

- 3 -

個股報告 
下半年營收、獲利逐季向上 
AI 伺服器、車用及小訊號產品訂單能見度佳，強茂 B/B ratio 值維持在 1.3~1.5 高檔
位置，強茂 8 月營收 13.76 億元，MoM-4.89%，YoY+29.82%，3Q26 新增部份產
能，加上產品調漲，預期營收及毛利率持續向上，4Q26 有機會切入另一家 CSP 供應
鏈，年底增加封測產能(小訊號為主)、越南 TOREX 預計在 1Q27 交割，整體而言營運
動能無虞。預估強茂 3Q26 營收 42.51 億元，QoQ+7.47%，YoY+30.02%，毛利率
34.44%，稅後淨利 5.58 億元，QoQ+14.39%，YoY+84.08%，EPS 1.46 元。 
歐美重心轉移、非紅供應鏈，台 IDM 廠有訂價權 
在 AI 需求強勁推動下 ， 單機櫃功耗持續上升並導入高壓直流電架構 ， 帶動功率元件量
價同步提升，應用於伺服器功率元件年複合成長率遠高於產業平均，在產能吃緊情況
下，歐美 IDM 廠將重心轉向利潤較佳的高壓、SiC 以及低壓大電流的 DrMOS 核心市
場，排擠中低壓功率元件產能。同時，安世、揚傑等中資背景企業面臨去中化，伺服
器、車用工控等領域加速將訂單轉向台廠。 
目前成熟製程與後段封裝產能極度吃緊，具備晶圓代工、晶片設計到封裝完整布局的 
強茂等 IDM 廠，憑藉產品線的廣度及垂直整合優勢，能確保供貨不中斷並免受外包
產能排擠 ， 更實質掌握了稀缺產能的訂價權 ， 在這一波由AI 架構升級與地緣政治雙軌
驅動的轉單潮中，將成為優先且最大受惠者。 
機櫃功率愈大，功率元件量價同步提升 
AI 應用佔強茂營收比重 11%，多數來自運算托盤(compute tray)上 Power MOSFET、
Schottky、TVS 或小訊號等功率產品，運算托盤上功率元件產值佔整機櫃超過一半，
主要核心在整合式供電模組(DrMOS)，DrMOS 技術門檻高，由歐美大廠主導，台廠
不易切入，但供電相數的增加，將同步提升對周邊功率元件的需求量。 
以 GB300 為例，單顆 GPU 功率 1.4kW，電壓 0.8V，核心負載輸出電流是 1,750A，
根據單顆高密度 DrMOS / SPS 晶片的最大承載電流(70A~130A)規格去設計供電相
數(Power Phase) ，每一相數都必須配置一套完整的硬體元件，當 GPU 功率提升至
2.3kW (Vera Rubin) ，GPU 功率增加 60%，供電相數及功率元件需求量亦將等幅放
大。 強茂功率元件已成功打入CSP 大廠 ， 未來將持續受惠運算托盤上功率元件量的提
升及質的改變(如 TVS 相對大電流，晶粒面積變大，單價提升)，產業成長趨勢明確。 
預估強茂 2027 年 EPS 6.95 元，建議買進，目標價 208 元 
投研部預估強茂 2026 年營收 159.39 億元，YoY+21.73%，稅後淨利 19.21 億元，
YoY+61.22%，EPS 5.03 元；預估 2027 年營收 183.38 億元，YoY+15.05%，稅後淨
利 26.57 億元，YoY+38.29%，EPS 6.95 元。目前強茂 PE 24X，2026 年最高本益比
已超越 2017~2018 年、2020~2022 年功率元件景氣循環 20X~30X 區間，AI 伺服
器的應用有助拉升功率元件產業及公司評價， 給予強茂買進 建議，目標價 208 元
(2027F PE 30X)。

### Page 4

- 4 -

## Document 059: 260914_daiwa_aspeed

Source PDF: all report pdf/260914_daiwa_aspeed.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-09-14
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We provide our update on Aspeed following its upbeat 4Q26 
revenue guidance on 4 September. We believe the strong guidance 
reaffirms our positive view that agentic AI has served as a catalyst for end 
demand for regular servers starting early 2026. Baseboard management 
controller (BMC) shipments for general servers (AI and non-AI together) 
should grow c.50% YoY in 2026. We believe that the new 4Q26 revenue 
guidance implies regular server shipment volume will increase c.50% YoY 
in 2027, hence the 36/34% uplift in our 2027/28E earnings forecasts.

What’s the impact: Solid regular server shipments ahead. Aspeed’s 
latest guidance range for its 4Q26 sales at TWD6.6-6.8bn implies 4Q26 
BMC shipments of c.12m. With this run-rate, total BMC shipments in 2027 
should be near c.50m, much higher than our previous estimate of 36.1m. 
We attribute the ongoing strength to agentic AI and AI-related general-
purpose demand. We forecast Aspeed’s BMC for general servers to 
increase to 27m in 2027E (from 18m in 2026E and 13m in 2025). At the 
same time, we expect BMC for AI servers to increase c.50% to 20m in 
2027E (up from 13m in 2026E and 5m in 2025). To sum up, we now 
forecast BMC shipments of 32.7m in 2026 and 47.6m in 2027 (+46% YoY 
in 2027). Based on our AI-related general server assumptions (excluding 
non-AI general servers), we expect the CPU:XPU ratio to rise to 1:1.3 in 
2027E (from 1:2.6 in 2026E). We expect a rising CPU:XPU ratio to become 
a key growth driver and share-price catalyst in the years after 2027. Due to 
the solid end demand, management is considering adjusting its pricing 
again from November to factor in the additional packaging cost, leading to 
our higher gross margin assumptions from 4Q26.

New growth opportunities. Following our visits to Aspeed’s booth at 
Computex 2026 and the OCP APAC conference, we believe Aspeed will 
continually maximise the dollar content per server board. Compared with 
last year’s version (embedded software-defined FPGA), Aspeed is 
integrating a Lattice FPGA into its next-generation Bridge IC (SMC; 
AST1840), which should support higher ASPs for its products.

What we recommend: We lift our 2026-28E EPS by 9-36% to factor in our 
higher BMC shipment assumptions. We lift our 12M TP to TWD26,660 
(from TWD19,800), based on an unchanged PER of 66x (high end of its 
past-3-year PER range of 21-78x), applied to our 1-year forward EPS 
forecast. Our 2025-28E EPS CAGR of 83% makes our target PER 
undemanding, given the resulting PEG ratio of 0.8. Key downside risk: 
weak AI server demand.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 6-26% higher than the consensus, 
likely due to our bullish view on Aspeed’s ASP and shipment story.

14 September 2026

Promising outlook on agentic AI

➢ Rising CPU:XPU ratio is becoming a key growth driver for Aspeed… 
➢ … which indicates solid regular server demand despite supply hiccups 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating and raising 12-month TP to TWD26,660

Company profile 
Established in November 2004, Aspeed is a fabless IC design company specialising in baseboard 
management controllers (BMCs), which are mostly used for the remote management of servers. Aspeed 
is a global leader in BMC, with about 70% market share. Its other products include PC/AV extension 
SoC, which supports the extension of digital signals of PCs or multimedia content to remote terminals; 
and Cupola360, which is a spherical image processor that supports 360-degree cameras.

### Page 5

5

ASPEED Technology (5274 TT): 14 September 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 14 Sep 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Aspeed’sboard consists of 9 directors, with 4 independent directors. The directors’backgrounds
appear to have a high correlation to semiconductors (either education or work experience),
which we see as a positive for executive quality. In 2025, Aspeed held 6 board meetings and the
average attendee rate was 100%. Given Aspeed’sChairman is also the CEO, we think it is a
negative factor for board/executive management as well as external shareholders.
Capital management 1
Aspeed declared a DPS of TWD80 in 2025, representing an 77% payout ratio. Being a leading
global board management controller (BMC) IC design vendor with c.70% market share globally,
the company’sleading position and asset-light model (major expenses are from R&D
employees) allow it to maintain high ROE/ROA ratios and a high dividend payout policy. BMC IC
recently penetrated new applications (such as power supply, storage, edge servers), which can
improve its profitability on a higher revenue scale, in our view.
Related party &
transaction 2
In 2025, there were no sales to or purchases from related parties. In previous years, the main
purchases from related parties was from Avago. After selling its fully-owned Emulex unit to
Aspeed, Avago (Broadcom) became an Aspeed's shareholder with a c.6% stake in 2016. We
believe the bargaining power from its 6% shareholding was not sufficient to preload its IC
products on Aspeed's products or earn unfair profits. In any case, this relationship ended in
February 2021. As such, we see limited risk from related-party transactions.
S Supply chain
management 1
Aspeed ensures that suppliers comply with social responsibility, green environmental protection,
occupational safety and health, human rights and RBA, and prefer to work with ISO9001,
ISO14001 and ISO45001 certified suppliers. In addition, every Aspeed supplier is required to
issue a publicly disclosed sustainability management report.
E Water & wastewater
management 1
Being a fabless IC design company, Aspeed’sbusiness doesn’tinvolve a production process.
According to the company's data, it produced 4,035 tonnes of wastewater in 2025. As such, we
see limited risk from its water and waste water management.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
Being a fabless IC design company, Aspeed’sbusiness doesn’tinvolve production processes
that may produce hazardous materials. That said, the company indicated it would employ green
design concepts and simplified product structures throughout its R&D process in order to reduce
pollution. As such, we see limited risk from its waste and hazardous materials management.
E GHG emissions 1
Due to its fabless business model, Aspeed produces limited emissions of GHG, wastewater, and
waste materials each year. According to the company’sdata, it produced around 273.5 tonnes of
CO2 in 2025.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 1

We hosted TECO (1504.TW, not covered) management at our Asia Leaders 
Conference 2026 (Aug 31 - Sep 2) in Hong Kong. Management is positive on their 
data center business, which includes construction services, products (e.g. 
transformer, busway, AC motor controls, etc.), and modular data center (MDC). 
Management remains positive on data center demand in Southeast Asia, highlighting 
a major CSP client’s 1GW (5-year) plan in Thailand, Malaysia, and potential in 
Indonesia in the future. The positive tone echoes our positive view on the AI 
infrastructure cycle, on which we raised our AI servers implied AI chips forecast by 
14% / 22% / 14% in 2026E-28E in June (report link) to +75% / 42% / 20% YoY in 
2026E-28E, or 19m / 27m / 32m in 2026E-28E. Capex estimates on leading US CSPs 
was also raised by 8% / 27% / 25% in 2026E-28E to +76% / +35% / +8% YoY in 
2026E-28E, or up to US$1.1tn by 2028E (report link). As we highlighted in our FII 
Chairman and Innolight Chairman visits takeaways, supply chain remains positive on 
rising AI investments in coming years, with spending across CSP , NeoCloud, 
Government, and Enterprise, and we are at an early stage of infrastructure spending 
and application development. End-customers are involved more in supply chain 
product development and capacity investment, also better reflecting real demand.  
Company profile: TECO (1504.TW, not covered) was founded in 1956, starting with 
motor manufacturing and is currently among the five largest industrial motor 
suppliers worldwide. The company mainly offers electromechanical systems, with 
three major business groups of Green Mechatronic Solutions, Intelligence Energy, 
and Air & Intelligent Life. Apart from its traditional motor business, TECO is 
expanding in new markets including ESCO low-carbon solutions, EV powertrain, 
offshore wind power, energy storage system and data center engineering. The 
company has built 700 MV of data centers in Southeast Asia. 
Key Takeaways

1. Data center business: Management remains positive on the business and stated 
they provide both construction services across power, cooling, and networking, and

### Page 2

related products (e.g. transformer, busway, AC motor controls, etc.). The company’s data
center business focuses on Southeast Asia, where a major customer (a leading US CSP)
announced a 1GW data center in Malaysia (announced in 2025), 1GW data center in
Thailand (announced in 2026), and potential new data center in Indonesia. Other than
leading US CSPs, the company also sees opportunities in sovereign AI, for example,
Japan is planning a 5-year 350mW AI data center, which could use MDC (modular data

center) for speedy deployment.
2. Modular Data Center (MDC) strength: Management highlights MDC saves AI data
center deployment time by ~50% to within 9 months, and reducing 15-20% capex, as it
moves most work into factory production, reducing onsite engineering. Modular Data
Center includes: modular IT server system, modular UPS system, modular power
distribution system, modular container genset system (power generation), and modular
cooling system, facilitating the AI data center deployment. The company’s product in

Malaysia is also for a leading US CSP , which one MDC could deliver 2.7mW.

## Document 067: 260914_nmr_chipbond-UG

Source PDF: all report pdf/260914_nmr_chipbond-UG.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-09-14
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

EQUITY: OSAT
Chipbond Technology Corporation   6147.TWO 6147 TT
Global Markets Research
14 September 2026
Concrete improvement in fundamentals in sight
Solid SiPh demand and future opportunities; baseline 
business benefiting from AI ripple effect; upgrade to Buy
Upgrade to Buy on increased progress in SiPh and potential DDIC backend reshuffles, 
with a new TP of TWD270, implying 43% upside
We were encouraged by Chipbond’s efforts to utilize its idle bumping capacity and make inroads 
into silicon photonics (SiPh) but did not upgrade the stock as we assumed that the market has 
likely priced in much more than it would deliver (report). Beside the reset in the market sentiment 
(share price -38% from the peak in late May vs. TAIEX +3%), we see increased progress in 
Chipbond’s bumping services in SiPh and that its baseline DDIC business could enjoy the “AI 
ripple effect” given a Korean DDIC vendor’s supply chain reshuffle. We lift our 2026/27/28F EPS 
from TWD4.55/5.71/ 6.70 to TWD5.77/9.04/10.60 (Fig. 2) . We upgrade Chipbond to Buy from 
Neutral and raise TP to TWD270 (from TWD171), which is based on 30x (unchanged)  2027F 
EPS of TWD9.04, at the high end of the company’s historical band (Fig. 4) to capture the 
diversification into SiPh. Chipbond currently trades at 21x 2027F EPS. Downside risks are softer-
than-expected optical communication demand and slower new project ramp-up.
TIA bumping remains the major driver in SiPh; TFLN engagement worth watching
While the addressable segments of Chipbond’s gold bumping include transimpedance amplifier 
(TIA), photodetector (PD), driver and modulator, we believe so far only TIA and PD are in 
production, and most of Chipbond’s SiPh revenue comes from 200G TIA bumping due to a 
larger die size than PD, with tie-ups with most of the major North American vendors. We estimate 
Chipbond’s SiPh revenue to grow 110%/70% in 2027-28F and contribute low-/high-teens of 
revenue vs. c.6% in 2026F; thus, a more significant mix benefit could support GM growth to 
32.7%/34.4% in 2027-28F from 29.4% in 2026F. Additionally, we believe Chipbond’s backend 
collaboration with UMC (2303 TT, Buy) in thin-film lithium niobate (TFLN) modulator production is 
worth watching given a much larger die size.
Share gain in LDDI backend might mitigate some device unit pressure
We expect Chipbond’s revenue growth to moderate in 4Q26F after +14% q-q growth in 3Q26F on a 
N American handset brand’s seasonal demand and SiPh resilience given continued PC/ Android 
demand headwinds to Chipbond’s baseline DDIC business (~80% of current revenue). However, 
we noted before that a Korea DDIC vendor could engage with UMC due to the company’s captive 
fab partner’s likely 8” capacity offloading amidst resource constraints  (report). The capacity 
optimization might cover more than fabrication but also gold bumping, and we think Chipbond could 
thus have the opportunity to gain LDDI backend share from 2027F in the earliest.

### Page 3

Company profile
Chipbond is one of the world's leading DDI OSAT players. Established in 1997, Chipbond specialises in providing display driver and radio-frequency
semiconductor packaging and testing services.
Valuation Methodology
Our TP of TWD270 is based on 30x 2027F EPS. Our target P/E of 30x is at the high end of the historical P/E range. TAIEX is the benchmark index.
Risks that may impede the achievement of the target price
Downside risks: 1) Softer-than-expected silicon photonic demand or fiercer competition, 2) slower-than-expected smartphone and TV demand, 3)

weaker-than-expected COF/tape demand.
ESG
Chipbond manages its corporate social responsibility by focusing on five dimensions: corporate governance, workplace of well-being, environmental
and sustainable development, customer service and supplier management, and social participation. The company sets water-saving milestones and
establishes low-carbon plants in practice.

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors
The rating system is a relative system, indicating expected performance against a specific benchmark identified for each individual stock, 
subject to limited management discretion. An analyst’s target price is an assessment of the current intrinsic fair value of the stock based on an 
appropriate valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow 
analysis, expected return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated 
target price, defined as (target price - current price)/current price.

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that 
the analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 
'N/A' are not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging 
Markets (ex-Asia): MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and 
by other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

China's advanced packaging industry remains a secular AI-driven

growth story. But, we see rising overcapacity risk and prefer
equipment vendors and market share gainers.
Advanced packaging is becoming increasingly critical to AI GPU performance,
particularly in China. As AI accelerators require greater compute density, memory
bandwidth, and system-level integration, advanced packaging provides a key
pathway to improve performance. This is particularly important for China, where
restrictions on access to leading-edge semiconductor  equipment constrain further

front-end process scaling, increasing the strategic value of packaging-led
performance improvements. We are therefore constructive on the structural growth
outlook for China advanced packaging.
However, we are more cautious than consensus on how this TAM growth
translates into OSAT earnings. We see a growing risk of moderate overcapacity, as
attractive industry growth and margins encourage incumbent OSATs and new
entrants to add 2.5D/3D capacity. Our bottom-up analysis suggests China’s 2.5D

capacity could rise from 120k wafers in 2025 to 436k in 2027, while effective
demand remains constrained  by advanced-node yields and HBM availability. We
estimate industry utilization at only ~63% in 2027, suggesting stronger competition
could weigh on pricing and limit margin upside even as industry revenue continues
to grow.
We therefore prefer advanced-packaging equipment vendors over broad-based
OSAT exposure.  The ongoing capacity race requires substantial upfront spending,

regardless of whether the resulting OSAT capacity ultimately generates attractive
returns. At the same time, the migration from conventional packaging toward
2.5D/3D raises process complexity and equipment intensity per unit of capacity,
providing an additional structural growth driver for equipment suppliers. Aggregate
OSAT capex is already accelerating, supporting our preference for capex
beneficiaries including  ASMPT and ACM Research among our covered names.

Within OSATs, we prefer share gainers: upgrade JCET to OW; initiate SJ Semi at
EW. We upgrade JCET to Overweight with a Rmb90.2 PT, as we expect the company
to emerge as a meaningful share gainer in domestic 2.5D packaging,  and continued
mix diversification toward computing and automotive. We initiate SJ Semi at Equal-
weight with a Rmb118.4 PT. While it remains China’s leading 2.5D packaging vendor,
we believe its premium valuation already discounts much of its technology

leadership and AI-driven growth, while increasing competition, customer
concentration and HBM availability create a more balanced risk-reward.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley
Research as only a single factor in making their investment

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

### Page 4

M
Asia Pacific Insight
4
Investment Summary
China advanced packaging: Structural AI growth, but value capture should favor
equipment vendors and OSAT share gainers. China's advanced packaging industry
remains a secular AI-driven growth story, but we see a growing divergence between
industry TAM growth and earnings capture across the value chain. Advanced packaging is
becoming increasingly critical to AI accelerator performance by enabling higher compute

density, memory bandwidth and heterogeneous integration through technologies
including wafer-level processing, RDL, TSV, chiplets, 2.5D integration, 3DIC and hybrid
bonding. This is particularly important in China, where restrictions on access to leading-
edge front-end semiconductor equipment increase the strategic value of packaging-led
performance improvements.
Exhibit 8: AI chip value chain (China vs. US) - we focus on China advanced packaging in this report

Source: Morgan Stanley Research
We expect China’s advanced packaging TAM to reach approximately Rmb100bn by
2029, implying a roughly 13% CAGR over 2025-29, while the chiplet / 2.5D segment
should grow substantially faster, reaching Rmb17.7bn by 2029 at a 40% CAGR. However,
we are more cautious than consensus on how this growth translates into OSAT earnings.
China’s 2.5D annual capacity is expected to expand from around 120k wafers in 2025 to
436k wafers in 2027, while effective demand is constrained by advanced-node yields, HBM

availability and customer qualification. We therefore estimate industry utilization at only
around 63% in 2027, suggesting moderate overcapacity could pressure pricing and limit
margin upside even as industry revenue continues to grow.

Three key debates
1. Will China's advanced packaging industry face oversupply? — We see
moderate overcapacity risk, particularly in 2.5D.
The market remains constructive on supply-demand, given strong domestic AI compute
growth, but we think capacity additions could run ahead of effective demand. Attractive

industry growth and margins are drawing incumbent OSATs and new entrants into
2.5D/3D packaging, while actual packaging demand is constrained by the availability of
qualified accelerator dies and HBM. In addition, a shift from CoWoS-S-like to CoWoS-L-like
architectures could create a capacity-mix mismatch, with excess S-like capacity even if L-
like capacity remains tight. Longer term, greater adoption of 3D integration could also
reduce the amount of 2.5D package footprint required per accelerator die

2. Who captures more value — OSATs or equipment vendors? — We
prefer equipment vendors.
Our key distinction is that OSAT earnings depend on capacity being filled, while
equipment revenues depend primarily on capacity being built. If our moderate-
overcapacity thesis is correct, aggressive OSAT expansion could dilute utilization and
pricing while still creating a strong order environment for equipment suppliers. The
technology transition also structurally increases equipment intensity: 2.5D/3D requires

additional fine-pitch RDL, TSV, wafer-level processing, temporary bonding/debonding,
high-precision die placement, TCB/hybrid bonding and advanced inspection. We therefore
expect equipment value per unit of packaging capacity to rise as China migrates toward
more complex architectures.
3. How much upside should investors assign to 3DIC, HBM packaging
and CPO? — Strategically important, but near-term earnings
contribution is likely overstated.
We view the current earnings opportunity as still primarily driven by 2.5D / CoWoS-like

capacity expansion, while 3DIC, HBM and CPO remain mainly medium- to long-term

### Page 6

M
Asia Pacific Insight
6
optionality. 3DIC could be particularly important in China as a workaround for constrained
front-end scaling, but high cost and compound-yield sensitivity remain key barriers.  We
estimate China’s 3DIC market may remain only in the tens of millions of US dollars over
the next 2-3 years. HBM is a large volume opportunity, but packaging value is still largely
retained within the memory ecosystem, limiting near-term independent OSAT value

capture. CPO has attractive long-term potential, but large-scale deployment is more likely
in 2027-28, implying limited contribution to China OSAT earnings over the next 1-2 years
Stock implications: Favor capex beneficiaries and share
gainers
We therefore see two preferred ways to gain exposure to China
advanced packaging.
First, equipment vendors offer the cleaner exposure to the capacity build-out. ACM
Research is a direct beneficiary through electrochemical plating, cleaning and other wet

processes, with advanced-packaging revenue already accelerating and panel-level
packaging moving closer to volume production. ASMPT is well positioned through TCB,
high-precision flip-chip and hybrid bonding, wherein increasing package size, finer
interconnect pitch and 3D integration should drive unit demand and mix toward higher-
value platforms.
Second, within OSATs, we prefer share gainers rather than broad industry exposure.
JCET is our preferred domestic OSAT as it is building 2.5D capacity from a low base,

improving customer visibility and expanding into computing, automotive, CPO and next-
generation packaging. We expect this to support structural share gains over the medium
term. By contrast, SJ Semi remains the domestic technology leader, with roughly 77%
share of China’s 2.5D market in 2025 and a clear lead in 3DIC commercialization, but we
believe its premium valuation already discounts much of this leadership. Competitive
capacity additions could also reduce its built-capacity share materially by 2027, while

customer concentration remains high.

### Page 8

M
Asia Pacific Insight
8
Debate 1: Will China's advanced packaging market face
oversupply?
Market view: Unlikely. China remains at the early stage of advanced packaging
adoption, while underlying demand is expected to grow rapidly, led by strong
domestic AI compute requirements. Looking ahead, structural growth in AI, data
centers, cloud computing, autonomous driving and high-end consumer electronics
should drive increasing adoption of advanced packaging. Against this backdrop, the

market expects demand growth to outpace capacity additions, supporting healthy
utilization and favorable industry economics for domestic OSATs.
Our view: Risk of moderate overcapacity. Firstly, more players, intensifying
competition,  and an attractive growth outlook is drawing more OSATs to enter the
market, which could drive capacity ramp-up ahead of demand increase. Secondly,
effective demand may be constrained by upstream bottlenecks, including yields at

advanced process nodes and the availability of HBM and related components.
Thirdly, as domestic accelerator chips migrate toward 3D integration, multiple
accelerator dies could be vertically stacked before being packaged alongside HBM.
This could reduce the amount of 2.5D packaging required per accelerator die
shipped assuming similar die sizes, as more accelerator dies can be integrated
within a given 2.5D package.
Where we could be wrong: 1) Domestic AI compute demand scales faster than

expected; 2) faster expansion of domestic advanced-node capacity and improved
domestic HBM availability; 3) the transition toward 3D integration is slower than
expected, resulting in a smaller reduction in 2.5D packaging demand.
Key metrics to monitor: 1) Domestic advanced packaging capacity; 2) domestic
advanced node capacity and HBM availability; 3) progress of domestic logic-die 3D
stacking technology.
We see a growing risk of moderate overcapacity, particularly in 2.5D packaging. While

we remain constructive on the structural growth of China’s advanced packaging market,
we think the market may be underestimating the pace of domestic capacity additions
relative to effective demand. Importantly, we distinguish between strong long-term TAM
growth and the near-/medium-term supply-demand balance. A rapidly growing industry
can still experience periods of excess capacity if supply is built ahead of customer
qualification, upstream component availability and actual accelerator shipments. We see

three reasons why this risk could become more visible over the next few years.
First, attractive growth prospects are encouraging increasingly aggressive capacity
expansion. Advanced packaging enjoys high gross margin and it has become a strategic
focus for incumbent OSATs and new entrants, with multiple domestic players (JCET,
Tongfu Micro, Huatian Technology, Forehope etc.) expanding 2.5D/3D-related capacity
simultaneously. Companies have an incentive to build ahead of confirmed demand, given

long equipment lead times and the strategic importance of securing domestic AI supply-
chain capacity. This creates a potential timing mismatch. Capacity can ramp relatively

### Page 9

M
Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 9
quickly once equipment is installed and qualified, while end-demand conversion depends
on customer product ramps and utilization.  Based on our bottom up analysis, we expect
China 2.5D packaging annual capacity to grow to 436k wafers in 2027, up from 120k
wafers in 2025, which is around 16% of non-China (TSMC mainly) capacity.

Second, on the demand side, upstream bottlenecks could constrain effective
packaging demand. The demand-side risk is that the end market demand for AI compute
may not translate directly into effective packaging demand. The immediate downstream
demand depends on the availability of qualified accelerator dies and HBM. Advanced-node
wafer yields are one potential constraint. Domestic AI accelerators increasingly require

sophisticated process technologies, and lower yields can reduce the number of known-
good dies available for packaging. We estimate AI GPU shipments by Chinese vendors will
reach 4.9mn units in 2027, up from 1.1mn units in 2025. Assuming around 21 chips per
wafer and overall yield of 85%, that indicates around 277k wafer demand in 2027.
HBM availability represents another major challenge. High-performance AI accelerators
increasingly depend on large amounts of high-bandwidth memory, and shortages in HBM

supply could effectively cap package output. In our CXMT initiation report, we estimated
3-4mn unit locally produced HBM units, but that could only support 0.75-1mn units of
GPU, with the rest still relying on foreign HBM supply. Other constraints, including on
substrates, advanced interconnect materials, thermal solutions and specialized equipment,
could have similar effects. In such a scenario, underlying AI demand could remain robust
while actual packaging utilization falls short of expectations. This distinction between end

demand and effective demand is central to our more cautious industry view.

Capacity mix is shifting from CoWoS-S-like to CoWoS-L-like solutions
Another important consideration is the mix of 2.5D packaging capacity being built in China.

Domestic advanced packaging players have generally started with CoWoS-S-like solutions
based on a full silicon interposer, while gradually developing more CoWoS-L-like
capabilities as AI packages become larger and more complex. This broadly mirrors the
technology evolution seen at TSMC, where CoWoS-S uses a silicon interposer, while
CoWoS-L combines an RDL-based interposer with local silicon interconnects to enable
greater package scalability and design flexibility. TSMC itself has shifted its development

focus toward CoWoS-L as HPC package sizes increase.
This transition could create a capacity-mix mismatch within China's advanced packaging
market. CoWoS-S-like capacity is currently more mature domestically and represents a
meaningful portion of existing and near-term capacity, whereas CoWoS-L-like capacity
remains at an earlier stage of ramp. At the same time, demand from newer generations of

### Page 11

M
Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 11
high-performance AI accelerators is increasingly moving toward larger and more complex
packages, which should favor CoWoS-L-like architectures over time. Recent industry
indications suggest domestic CoWoS-L-like products are beginning to enter volume
production, while the broader industry is increasingly treating CoWoS-L as the next major
technology upgrade for 2.5D packaging.
As a result, we see greater overcapacity risk in CoWoS-S-like capacity than in CoWoS-L-

like capacity. S-like demand could begin to moderate as customers migrate toward L-like
solutions, potentially leaving some dedicated S-like capacity underutilized even as
aggregate advanced packaging demand continues to grow. This distinction is important
because headline 2.5D capacity numbers may overstate the supply that is actually suitable
for next-generation AI accelerators. A market can therefore simultaneously experience
excess S-like capacity and relatively tight L-like capacity.

Domestic players are partly mitigating this risk by building flexibility into portions of their
capacity. Where common process modules can support both S-like and L-like flows,
capacity initially used for S-like products can potentially be reconfigured toward L-like
production as customer demand migrates. This should reduce the risk that all S-like
capacity becomes structurally stranded. However, the conversion is unlikely to be
completely frictionless, given differences in interposer architecture, process flow,

equipment requirements and customer qualification. Therefore, nominally flexible capacity
should not necessarily be treated as immediately interchangeable between the two
technologies.
The implication for our overcapacity thesis is that the risk may increasingly be one of
capacity mix rather than aggregate capacity alone. In the near term, the faster build-out of
S-like capacity could result in declining utilization as demand begins to shift toward L-like

solutions. Over the medium term, continued growth in L-like demand and the gradual
conversion of flexible lines could absorb part of this excess. We would therefore monitor
S-like and L-like capacity, utilization and customer qualifications separately, rather than
relying solely on aggregate domestic CoWoS-like capacity when assessing the industry's
supply-demand balance.

Also, the transition from 2.5D toward 3D integration could change the relationship
between accelerator shipments and 2.5D packaging demand. Today, a typical high-end

accelerator architecture places compute die(s) and HBM side-by-side on an interposer or
equivalent 2.5D platform. As domestic accelerator vendors move toward more advanced
3D architectures, multiple accelerator dies could increasingly be vertically integrated
before being packaged alongside HBM. Assuming broadly similar die sizes, this could allow
more compute dies to be incorporated within a given 2.5D package footprint, meaning
2.5D packaging demand may grow more slowly than do accelerator die shipments. In other

words, rising AI accelerator volumes do not necessarily translate linearly into rising 2.5D
packaging units or area over the longer term.
Conclusion
We remain constructive on the structural growth outlook for China's advanced packaging
market, while seeing a risk of moderate overcapacity as capacity investment accelerates.
The market's bullish view is well supported by rapidly growing domestic AI compute
demand, continued semiconductor localization and the increasing importance of advanced

packaging in enabling higher system-level performance. Our concern is that effective
packaging demand may grow more slowly than AI demand due to component constraints,
while industry capacity could expand relatively quickly.
Packaging architecture adds another layer of uncertainty. If domestic AI accelerators
increasingly adopt 3D integration, vertically stacking multiple GPU dies could reduce the
amount of 2.5D package footprint required per GPU die, assuming broadly stable die sizes

and total die volumes.

Debate 2: Which vendors are the biggest beneficiaries of
China's advanced packaging business?
Market view: OSAT vendors. The market increasingly views advanced packaging
as a key technology enabler for domestic AI GPUs, particularly as China’s access to
advanced node semiconductor production equipment remains constrained. As
further transistor scaling becomes more challenging, advanced packaging provides

an alternative pathway to enhance system-level performance through chiplets,
higher integration density, and higher-bandwidth interconnects. Against this
backdrop, OSAT vendors could emerge as key beneficiaries of China’s AI compute
build-out.
Our view: Equipment vendors. As we discussed in Debate 1, we expect China's
advanced packaging to be in a moderate degree of overcapacity over the next few
years, so the  broad-based capacity build-out could intensify competition, pressure

pricing, and constrain gross margin upside. Equipment vendors, by contrast, are
better positioned to capture the current capacity expansion cycle. Regardless of
whether incremental OSAT capacity ultimately generates attractive returns, the
initial capacity build-out requires meaningful equipment investment.
Metrics to monitor: 1) Capex of OSAT vendors; 2) margin trend of OSAT vendors;
3) revenue and earnings  growth of OSAT vendors.
Where we could be wrong: 1) Stronger China AI compute demand  than our

expectation, outpacing the advanced packaging capacity addition,  leading to higher
gross margin for OSAT vendors; 2) technological breakthrough of OSAT vendors,
leading to cost saving and margin expansion.
Equipment vendors are better positioned than OSAT vendors to play
China's advanced packaging
In our report on  JCET: Slow recovery with share loss; downgrade to UW (Sep 24, 2024),
we had already argued that the biggest beneficiaries of China's advanced packaging would

be the equipment vendors, rather than the OSAT vendors. Our main argument was that
the advanced packaging opportunity for OSAT vendors comes at the expense of higher
R&D and capex, weighing on near-term earnings, while the equipment vendors are the
beneficiaries from the capex build-up.
What has changed since then is the volume ramp-up of China's AI GPU shipments, and the
rising demand for domestic 2.5D advanced packaging demand. As discussed in Debate 1,

we see moderate overcapacity as a result, which may intensify competition, pressure
pricing, and constrain gross margin upside for OSAT vendors.
Equipment vendors, by contrast, should benefit more directly from the capacity
expansion itself. Regardless of whether incremental OSAT capacity ultimately earns an
attractive return, building that capacity requires upfront investment in bonding,

### Page 14

M
Asia Pacific Insight
14
lithography, deposition, plating, inspection/metrology, wafer handling and other back-end
equipment. In other words, OSAT earnings depend on capacity being filled, while
equipment revenues depend primarily on capacity being built. This distinction becomes
particularly important if our moderate-overcapacity thesis is correct. Aggressive OSAT
expansion could be dilutive to industry utilization and pricing while simultaneously

creating a strong order environment for equipment suppliers.

Advanced packaging should also be more equipment-intensive than conventional
packaging, providing a second leg to the equipment thesis. The transition toward

2.5D/3D integration requires additional and increasingly sophisticated process steps,
including fine-pitch RDL, TSV, wafer-level processing, temporary bonding/debonding, high-
precision die placement, thermal compression/hybrid bonding and advanced inspection. As
interconnect pitches shrink and package complexity increases, equipment performance
becomes increasingly critical to yield and throughput. We therefore expect equipment
value per unit of packaging capacity to rise as the domestic industry migrates from

conventional packaging toward 2.5D and ultimately 3D integration.
Identifying the key companies among equipment vendors

### Page 15

M
Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 15
Exhibit 20:  China's advanced packaging process and key companies
Source: Morgan Stanley Research
ACM Research (ACMR.O, OW): ACM Research is one of the more direct beneficiaries of
China’s advanced-packaging capacity build-out because its portfolio addresses several
process steps whose equipment intensity rises materially in wafer-level, 2.5D/3D and
panel-level packaging, particularly electrochemical plating, cleaning and other wet

processes. The exposure is already showing up in reported results: in Q2 2026, ACM’s
advanced-packaging revenue excluding ECP grew 153% YoY, while its ECP, furnace and
other technologies category grew 168% YoY, with management citing ECP and advanced
packaging as the key growth drivers. ACM shipped its 2,000th ECP chamber during the
quarter, demonstrating an increasingly meaningful installed base across logic, memory and
3D packaging. More importantly for the China capacity-build thesis, ACM received its first

production order for a 510×515mm horizontal panel-level electroplating system from an
existing advanced-packaging customer in mainland China, moving its panel-level offering
closer to volume production. As Chinese OSATs and integrated manufacturers expand
2.5D/3D, fan-out and eventually larger-format panel-level packaging, ACM should benefit
from both additional tool demand and increasing process intensity per package, while its
local manufacturing, application-development and service infrastructure should support

further localization-driven share gains.
ASMPT (0522.HK, OW):  ASMPT should be a major beneficiary of China’s advanced-
packaging investment through its leading positions in thermo-compression bonding (TCB),

### Page 16

M
Asia Pacific Insight
16
high-precision flip-chip die bonding and hybrid bonding, technologies that become
increasingly critical as AI processors migrate toward larger 2.5D packages, chiplets, HBM
and higher-density 3D integration. The operating momentum is already substantial:
ASMPT’s TCB revenue grew approximately 146% YoY in 2025, while its advanced-
packaging business reached a record US$339 million of revenue in 1H 2026, up 17% YoY
and representing 30% of group revenue. In July 2026, it received bulk orders for more

than 50 chip-to-substrate TCB tools from OSAT customers, highlighting the step-up in
equipment requirements as compound die sizes increase for AI workloads. China provides
an additional demand vector: ASMPT has reported particularly strong Chinese demand for
die- and wire-bonding equipment as domestic AI infrastructure investment and OSAT
utilization increase. As Chinese packaging houses move from conventional assembly
toward larger packages, finer-pitch interconnects, chip-on-wafer/chip-on-panel

architectures and ultimately more TCB and hybrid bonding, equipment value per unit of
packaging capacity should rise, creating a favorable mix shift toward ASMPT’s highest-
value advanced bonding platforms.
Other major domestic Chinese equipment companies: These span nearly the full
advanced-packaging process flow. Circuit Fabology Microelectronics Equipment, or
CFMEE (688630.SH; 9630.HK; not covered), provides direct-write lithography for RDL,
bumping, TSV and system-on-wafer applications. Its wafer-level packaging tools have

secured orders from leading domestic OSATs for large AI packages including CoWoS-like
architectures, while its large-format panel-level lithography platform has progressed into
customer qualification and commercial orders. Kingsemi (688037.SH; not covered)
supplies coat/develop, wafer cleaning, temporary bonding and laser debonding systems
specifically designed for advanced packaging and 2.5D/3D integration, giving it exposure to
thick-photoresist RDL processes, TSV cleaning and the temporary bond/debond steps

required for aggressive wafer thinning. Hwatsing Technology (688120.SH; not covered) is
leveraged to CMP, ultra-precision wafer thinning, dicing and wet processing, with its
thinning equipment already receiving orders in advanced-memory and Chiplet applications;
these steps become increasingly demanding as wafers and dies are thinned for stacked
architectures. Skyverse Technology (688361.SH; not covered) provides inspection and
metrology equipment, including 3D topography measurement capable of monitoring fine-

line RDL and TSV-related processes for HBM and 2.5D/3D packaging, a business which
could grow through the increase in process-control intensity as package complexity rises.
Piotech (688072.SH; not covered) is exposed to the next stage of packaging localization
through wafer-to-wafer and die-to-wafer hybrid bonding, surface activation and bonding
metrology. Its product portfolio explicitly targets HBM and 3D integration as China moves
from conventional packaging localization toward domestic production of the most

advanced bonding steps. Collectively, the industry  should benefit from the absolute
increase in Chinese advanced-packaging capacity and the rising number, precision and
value of process steps required per package as the industry migrates toward Chiplets,
HBM, 2.5D/3D integration and panel-level packaging.
BE Semiconductor (BESI.AS, OW, covered by Nigel van Putten): Besi is particularly well
positioned for China’s move into higher-end 2.5D and 3D packaging because its core

franchise is concentrated in the high-accuracy die-placement technologies that become
increasingly important as interconnect pitches shrink, including advanced flip-chip,
thermo-compression and hybrid bonding. The China linkage is unusually explicit: Besi has
cited a “significant increase” in demand from Chinese subcontractors as the country builds

### Page 17

M
Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 17
out AI-related infrastructure, while its 2025 annual report identified seven planned
advanced-packaging fab projects in China representing approximately US$6.3 billion of
investment. This capacity expansion coincides with a favorable technology transition for
Besi, as customer roadmaps point toward greater adoption of hybrid bonding and TC Next
across HBM4/4E, ASICs, high-performance computing and co-packaged optics. Current

results already demonstrate the strength of this cycle, with Q2 2026 revenue up 69% YoY
and orders up 129% YoY, driven particularly by hybrid bonding, datacenter and other AI-
related applications. China therefore offers Besi two layers of upside: more absolute
advanced-packaging capacity and a shift toward increasingly precise, equipment-intensive
bonding architectures where Besi has its strongest competitive positioning. Key risks: 1)
hybrid bonding adoption more limited than expected; 2) strength in core business fades; 3)

FX headwinds pressure margins
Among Japanese vendors, DISCO (6146.T; OW; covered by Suzune Tamura) is one of
the clearest beneficiaries through its leading position in precision wafer grinding, thinning
and dicing, process steps that become increasingly important as HBM, chiplets and
2.5D/3D architectures require thinner wafers and more complex singulation. Its portfolio
extends to large-format panel dicing as fan-out and panel-level packaging scale. Key risks:

1) sharp slowdown in AI semiconductor investment; 2) weakening in consumer demand.
TOWA (6315.T; EW; covered by Yoshihito Hasegawa) is similarly well positioned through
compression molding and molded-underfill equipment used in HBM, chiplet, 2.5D/3D and
panel-level packaging, with continued investment by Asian OSATs and memory
manufacturers supporting demand for its higher-end molding platforms. TOWA, which has
strong competitiveness in molding equipment, recently began sales of its next-generation

compression molding system, INNOMS. The system was developed with the goal of
reducing mass production costs by approximately 50%. Compared with conventional
models, it reduces installation space by 40%, power consumption by around 50%, and
consumables usage by approximately 25%. The company plans to deploy the system
across a wide range of applications, including memory and logic devices. Key risks:
stagnant chip/SPE market; heightened geopolitical risk; reduced China demand; stall in

tech advances; slow sales expansion for cutting-edge package/HBM equipment, loss of
tech edge due to market changes, margin decline.
Kulicke & Soffa (NASDAQ: KLIC; not covered) offers thermo-compression and emerging
hybrid-bonding platforms for logic and HBM and has an established Chinese OSAT
customer base. The geographic mix of its current advanced-bonding orders is not
disclosed.

Debate 3: Besides 2.5D packaging, how much upside can
emerging advanced-packaging technologies contribute to
China OSATs?
Market view: 3DIC, HBM packaging, CPO could be meaningful drivers. The
market is increasingly pricing in meaningful incremental opportunities for Chinese
OSATs from next-generation packaging technologies including 3DIC, HBM and CPO,
with each potentially creating a sizable new revenue pool as domestic AI

infrastructure scales.
Our view:  More cautious on the near-term earnings contribution. While all three
technologies are strategically important, we believe commercialization remains
constrained by either cost, customer ownership of packaging, or technology
maturity. As a result, we see the current advanced-packaging earnings story as
being driven primarily by 2.5D/CoWoS-type capacity expansion, while 3DIC, HBM
and CPO remain mostly medium- to long-term optionality.

Metrics to monitor: 1) Customer qualification and volume-production milestones;
2) dedicated capacity additions; 3) utilization rates; and 4) disclosed revenue
contribution from these new technologies.
Where we could be wrong: 1) OSAT technology breakthrough which lower the
cost of 3DIC; 2) accelerating HBM outsourcing together with OSAT HBM packaging
capability improvement; 3) CPO yield improves faster than expected together with
OSAT CPO packaging capability improvement.

3DIC
3DIC – China could over-index in adoption, but high cost remains the key constraint.
TSMC remains the global leader in 3DIC through its SoIC platform, although SoIC capacity
is still small relative to its CoWoS footprint. AMD and Apple are the major customers of
TSMC SoIC capacity with Nvidia's share increasing next year. China, meanwhile, is seeing a
growing number of early 3DIC implementations across edge AI and mobile.
• Rockchip’s RK1820 integrates high-bandwidth on-chip DRAM with an NPU, with

industry sources describing the memory implementation as a 3D-stacked
architecture;
• Xiaomi’s recently announced XRING O100 AI accelerator reportedly uses 6nm
wafer-to-wafer stacking and hybrid bonding to deliver 1.22TB/s memory bandwidth
• Dongfang Suanxin’s DF1000 adopts DRAM-on-logic wafer-level hybrid bonding for
near-memory AI computing.
•  Huawei launched the Kirin 9050 Pro in September 2026 for the Mate XT 2, the
first high-performance processor to use logic folding technology. The design stacks

logic units across layers and connects them vertically, shortening signal paths to
reduce latency and improve performance.

### Page 19

M
Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 19
We view Kirin 9050 Pro as potentially the most meaningful domestic proof point given its
deployment in a commercial flagship smartphone, the annual sell-through of Huawei Mate
XT (using Kirin 9020 chip) is around 300-400k unit in past year. Therefore, we estimate
China’s 3DIC market will remain at only tens of millions of US dollars over the next 2–3
years. A meaningful expansion in TAM would require either tens of millions of units in

consumer electronics applications or million-unit volumes of high-ASP AI accelerators.
Exhibit 21: LogicFolding for Huawei's mobile SoC
Source:  Huawei.
The key limitation, in our view, is cost. A true 3D logic stack adds substantially more
manufacturing content than conventional packaging: additional active dies/wafers must be
fabricated and tested, followed by wafer thinning, CMP/surface preparation, TSV or
backside interconnect processing, high-precision alignment, hybrid bonding and

significantly more inspection/metrology. More importantly, the economics are highly
sensitive to compound yield—a defective die or poor bond can destroy value already
embedded in another known-good die. Industry cost studies therefore identify incoming
wafer cost and yield loss as major drivers of 3D hybrid-bonding economics, rather than the
bonding tool itself. This makes 3DIC very different from conventional OSAT packaging.
The package is increasingly incorporating fab-like process steps and expensive processed

silicon, meaning the absolute packaging/integration cost can rise materially even if hybrid-
bonding unit costs decline with scale. This is also why global adoption has so far
concentrated in products for which performance per watt, bandwidth or form factor can
justify the premium—AMD HPC/AI, Apple’s high-end M-series and, increasingly, high-value
AI accelerators—rather than broad-based semiconductor applications.

China could nevertheless adopt 3DIC more aggressively than global economics alone

would imply, as advanced packaging partly substitutes for constrained front-end
scaling. Huawei’s -scaling roadmap is the clearest example: Rather than relying purely on
geometric transistor scaling, Huawei is explicitly using LogicFolding to shorten signal paths
and increase effective transistor density, with the company targeting density equivalent to
a 1.4nm-class process by 2031 through system-, circuit- and packaging-level innovation.
This is particularly relevant in China, where restricted access to EUV and leading-edge

process technology makes architectural and packaging workarounds comparatively more
valuable.
HBM
HBM – strong volume opportunity, but limited incremental value capture for OSATs
for now. HBM is arguably the largest potential packaging-related demand pool given rapid
AI accelerator growth, but the key debate is who captures the packaging value. CXMT is
currently developing domestic HBM capabilities. In our CXMT initiation report, we
estimate CXMT can produce 3-4mn units of HBM3E stacks in 2027. However, packaging is

currently largely retained within the memory ecosystem, including through affiliated
packaging capabilities such as Xinputianying, limiting the immediate addressable market
for independent OSATs. Outsourcing could increase as technology matures and volumes
scale, but we would view this primarily as a volume-transfer opportunity rather than a
major structural uplift in OSAT value capture unless independent OSATs are able to take
on higher-value TSV / stacking / advanced integration steps.

### Page 21

Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 21
Exhibit 24: CXMT to ramp up HBM capacity from 20k wpm in

Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research estimates.
CPO – attractive long-term TAM, but unlikely to move near-term earnings. CPO could
eventually create a meaningful new integration opportunity for OSATs as AI clusters

migrate toward higher-bandwidth and lower-power optical interconnects. However, the
technology remains relatively immature, with fiber alignment, thermal management,
testing and supply-chain integration still key bottlenecks. Industry forecasts generally
point to initial volume production beginning around 2026, but large-scale deployment is
more likely in 2027–28, suggesting limited contribution to China OSAT earnings over the
next 1–2 years. We therefore see CPO as a credible long-term optionality rather than a

near-term earnings catalyst.

JCET: Upgrade to Overweight; advanced                        packaging share
gain and business diversification
JCET to emerge as a meaningful share gainer in China’s 2.5D/CoWoS-like advanced
packaging.
• JCET is effectively building its 2.5D business from a low base, leaving substantial
room for incremental share gains in a market currently highly concentrated: SJ
Semiconductor (SJSemi) held c.85% of China’s 2.5D packaging market by revenue in

2024. JCET Microelectronics (Jiangyin), the group’s key high-end advanced
packaging facility, only began volume ramp-up recently, but the trajectory is
encouraging. Revenue reached Rmb212mn in 1H26, while net losses narrowed to
Rmb89mn, with utilization continuing to improve. Management expects the
business to turn profitable in 2027, while we estimate capacity could reach
~5kwpm by end-2027, providing meaningful headroom for 2.5D revenue and share

expansion.
• Customer visibility is improving: JCET  already has an anchor CSP customer, while
qualification and ramp-up with additional customers are progressing. Management
has highlighted a rich pipeline of customer projects and expects the Jiangyin 2.5D
business to scale to Rmb billions of annual revenue over the next 1–2 years.
•  JCET is broadening its advanced-packaging footprint beyond its existing Jiangyin
capacity. The recently announced Rmb7.8bn high-end advanced packaging facility in

Shanghai Lingang should provide a second leg of capacity growth, with the
company also building capabilities across CPO, panel-level packaging, 2.5D/3D
integration and other next-generation technologies.
Taken together, we see JCET transitioning from a relatively small participant to an
increasingly scaled supplier in domestic 2.5D advanced packaging, supported by capacity
ramp, customer diversification and technology expansion, which should translate into

structural market-share gains over the medium to long term.

JCET is accelerating its business mix diversification away from traditional
communications and consumer electronics toward higher-growth, higher-value-added
computing and automotive applications. In 1H26, computing revenue grew 40.4% YoY,

with its revenue contribution rising sharply to 30% from 22% in 1H25, making computing
JCET’s largest end-market for the first time. Automotive revenue increased 25.0% YoY to
Rmb2bn, with its revenue mix expanding to 11% from 9%. We expect this diversification to
continue. JCET’s dedicated Lingang automotive packaging facility commenced production
in March 2026 and entered volume production in 2Q26. As equipment installation,

customer qualification and product ramps progress, incremental capacity should support a
continued increase in automotive revenue contribution. In computing, JCET is stepping up
capacity and R&D investment across the AI data-center value chain, covering compute,
power, storage and interconnect, while continuing to scale advanced packaging platforms
such as XDFOI and developing next-generation technologies including CPO.

Domestic market share gain driven by mix upgrade, rather than price-led competition.

In 2025, JCET’s China revenue increased ~22% YoY to Rmb8.3bn,  outpacing domestic
revenue growth at key OSAT peers, including Tongfu Microelectronics at 15% YoY  and
Huatian Technology at 18% YoY. More importantly, JCET achieved this above-peer growth
alongside a 6.6ppt YoY expansion in domestic gross margin to 20.4%, versus only modest
margin improvement at Huatian and broadly flat/down margins at Tongfu. This
combination of accelerating revenue growth and sharply improving profitability suggests

that JCET is not gaining share through aggressive pricing. Rather, we believe it points to
structural share gains in higher-value-added segments such as computing, automotive, and
industrial applications.

Key downside risks to our OW view
• The global economy heads into recession, which leads to deteriorating demand for

smartphones, PCs and consumer electronics.
• JCET has limited technology breakthrough, which leads to the slower ramp-up of
its 2.5D/3D advanced packaging.
• Peers become more aggressive on pricing and JCET loses market share among local
customers.

JCET: Valuation methodology
We raise our price target to Rmb90.2: This reflects the changes in our earnings estimates
and increased intermediate growth rate to 19.0% up from 13.5% mainly because of

increasing mix from AI-related advanced packaging. We continue to derive our price target
using the base-case value from a residual income (RI) model. All of our key assumptions
are unchanged, including a cost of equity of 8.6% (beta of 1.2, risk premium of 5.5%, and
risk-free rate of 2.0%), and a terminal growth rate of 4.0%.

Asia Pacific Insight
30
SJ Semi: Leading advanced packaging player, with premium
valuation
We initiate coverage on SJ Semi with Equal-weight and a price target of Rmb118.4. SJ
Semi is a leading advanced packaging vendor in China and the No.1 player in domestic 2.5D
packaging, with c.77% market share in 2025. We expect the company to be a key
beneficiary of strong China AI GPU demand and increasing adoption of chiplet/
heterogeneous integration, driving 25% revenue CAGR over 2025-28, with a rising 2.5D

mix supporting a structurally superior margin profile versus domestic OSAT peers.
However, we see a more balanced risk-reward at current valuation. Aggressive capacity
expansion by domestic peers could pressure SJ Semi’s market share, pricing, and
utilization, while high customer concentration and potential bottlenecks in  HBM supply
add uncertainty to the pace of growth. We believe the stock’s premium valuation already
reflects its technology leadership and strong advanced-packaging growth outlook,

supporting our Equal-weight rating
Investment Positives
A key beneficiary of China’s advanced packaging growth. We see SJ Semi as a key
beneficiary of the rapid development of China’s AI computing ecosystem, in which
advanced packaging has become an increasingly critical part of the AI GPU supply chain as
chiplet architectures and heterogeneous integration gain adoption. According to CIC,
China’s advanced packaging market is expected to grow to Rmb100bn in 2029 at 13%

CAGR (2025-29), while the chiplet/multi-chip integration segment is expected to expand
to Rmb17.7bn in 2029 at 40% CAGR (2025-29). Beyond AI GPUs, advanced packaging
demand is also growing across data centers, autonomous driving, smartphones and
communications applications, providing SJ Semi with diversified structural growth drivers.
Given its leading position in China’s 2.5D packaging market, we expect SJ Semi to capture
this strong industry beta and deliver 25% revenue CAGR over 2025-28.

Leading position in China’s advanced packaging market with a structurally superior
margin profile. SJ Semi has established a leading position in China’s advanced packaging

### Page 31

M
Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 31
market, particularly in 2.5D packaging. In particular, the company ranked No.1 in China’s
2.5D packaging market with c.77% market share in 2025, while its 12-inch bumping
business also ranked No.1 in China, underscoring its leadership across key advanced
packaging platforms. We believe SJ Semi is well positioned to benefit from the
accelerating adoption of 2.5D/3D packaging driven by AI/HPC demand. Importantly, the

increasing revenue mix from 2.5D packaging should support a structurally higher gross
margin versus local OSAT peers, given the higher technical barriers and value-add of 2.5D
packaging. SJ Semi delivered a c.30% gross margin in 1H26, materially above major
domestic peers such as JCET, Tongfu Microelectronics and Huatian Technology, which
were generally in the low-to-mid teens. We expect continued 2.5D ramp-up to drive
revenue growth and sustained margin outperformance versus domestic peers.

Leading domestic 3DIC commercialization with a clear first-mover advantage. We

believe SJ Semi is currently the most advanced among domestic OSAT peers in 3DIC, with
its micro-bump-based SmartPoser®-3DIC-BP platform already in volume production and its
hybrid-bonding SmartPoser®-3DIC-HB platform advancing C2W/W2W capabilities. This
positions the company well for the next generation of AI accelerators, for which increasing
compute density and bandwidth requirements — including architectures implied by
Huawei’s -scaling roadmap — should drive greater adoption of 3D integration. SJ Semi is

moving aggressively on capacity, with its Rmb10bn Shanghai Lingang 3DIC project
commencing construction in June 2026. While front-end foundries may have stronger
underlying process capabilities, we believe SJ Semi is ahead in 3DIC commercialization and
dedicated capacity deployment, supporting a meaningful first-mover advantage as
domestic 3DIC demand scales.

Investment Concerns
Intensifying competition in advanced packaging could pressure market share and

returns. While SJ Semiconductor currently holds a leading position in China’s advanced
packaging market, competition is likely to intensify as domestic OSAT peers, including
JCET, Tongfu Microelectronics, and Forehope Electronic, accelerate capacity expansion and
technology upgrades in advanced packaging. As competing capacity comes online and
peers narrow the technology gap, SJ Semiconductor could face greater pressure on
customer share, pricing and capacity utilization. In addition, aggressive industry-wide

capacity additions could increase the risk of oversupply, potentially weighing on margins
and returns on invested capital over the medium term.
High customer concentration creates a potential long-term risk. SJ Semiconductor has
significant revenue exposure to Huawei, ~74% in 1H25, making its earnings sensitive to
Huawei’s outsourcing strategy. Meanwhile, Huawei is developing its own advanced
packaging capabilities through Quliang and Ruixin. As these in-house platforms scale and

mature, Huawei could potentially shift part of its advanced packaging demand away from
external suppliers such as SJ Semiconductor. While this may not be an immediate risk
given capacity and technology constraints, greater internalization over time could reduce
SJ Semiconductor’s order visibility, utilization rates and bargaining power..
